$A\mapsto A^\#$ in the first realization and then $B\mapsto B^\flat$ in the second.
Hence the partially ordered collection of class realizations is preserved, not
erased. This gives an exact interface for transferring questions about class
realizations to numerical foundations; compare the general model-theoretic study in
\cite{WilliamsDiss}. (Merge, spelling out the reverse direction: $A^\#$ is defined from $A$ and
$\operatorname{col}$ by an elementary formula, and $\operatorname{col}$ is the same
set-theoretic relation in both realizations, which have the same sets; so
$A^\#\in\mathcal Y_1\subseteq\mathcal Y_2$ and $(A^\#)^\flat=A$ computed in the
second realization puts $A$ in $\mathcal X_2$.)

\subsection{Variants and limitations}

The same construction works for $\GB$ with set Choice but without Global Choice,
that is $\GBc$, deleting the Global Choice requirement from the translated source
theory. The present ordinal-graph codec still uses set Choice; $\GB$ without it
needs a different analysis. (Source~13.)

For $\KM$ with a $\ZFC$ set part, add impredicative numerical class comprehension
and translate the corresponding $\KM$ axioms. The same maps prove the analogous
bi-interpretation. Class quantifiers in the numerical comprehension scheme are
translated to class quantifiers in the source, where full comprehension is
available. Global Choice is included exactly when it is included in the chosen
source version. (Source~13.)

Further class principles, such as elementary transfinite recursion, can be
transported in the same manner by adding their explicit translations. Determining
short native formulations of those principles is a separate task. (Source~13.)

Source 13 sketched these three variants (``the same maps prove''); source 14's
\cref{hset:sf:thm:classbi} states them with the argument of its last paragraph.
The merge makes them a corollary with a proof.

\begin{corollary}[Class variants; added in this merge]\label{hset:sf:cor:variants}
Let $\Dd=\No$ or $\Oz$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Over $\GBc$: the theory $\Tth^{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm c}_{\Dd}$, defined as in
\cref{hset:sf:def:classaxioms} with $\GBc$ in place of $\GBC$ in item 4, is
bi-interpretable with $\GBc$.
\item For every set $\Sigma$ of sentences or schemes in the language of class
theory, in particular for Global Choice and for elementary transfinite recursion
$\ETR$, the extension of the numerical class theory by the translations
$\Sigma^{\I}$ is bi-interpretable with the corresponding extension of $\GBc$ or
$\GBC$.
\item Let $\Tth^{\mathrm{KM}}_{\Dd}$ be $\Tth^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Dd}$ with item 3
strengthened to \emph{impredicative} numerical comprehension (formulas with bound
class quantifiers). Then $\Tth^{\mathrm{KM}}_{\Dd}\bi\KM$ (with Global Choice), and
likewise without Global Choice over the $\GBc$ variant.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
(i) The proof of \cref{hset:sf:thm:classlift} (equivalently of
\cref{hset:sf:thm:classbi}) uses, on the source side, elementary comprehension,
class extensionality and the surjectivity of collapse, which needs set Choice
(\cref{hset:sf:prop:hwo}); Global Choice enters only as one of the translated axioms.
(ii) is \cref{hset:sf:lem:persist} applied to (i) or to
\cref{hset:sf:thm:classlift}.

(iii) By (ii), $\KM$ is bi-interpretable with the numerical class theory plus the
translations of the $\KM$ comprehension instances; it remains to see that this
extension and $\Tth^{\mathrm{KM}}_{\Dd}$ have the same theorems. In the forward
model, a native impredicative instance $\{n\in N:\varphi(n,\bar p,\bar B)\}$, with
class quantifiers over subclasses of $N$, is translated to a set-theoretic formula
whose class quantifiers range over classes contained in $N$; $\KM$ comprehension
gives the class, so the forward interpretation satisfies impredicative numerical
comprehension and $\KM$ interprets $\Tth^{\mathrm{KM}}_{\Dd}$. Conversely, a
translated $\KM$ instance asks for a saturated $B$ with
$\forall c\in\UC\,(c\in_NB\leftrightarrow\psi^{\I}(c,\ldots))$, where the class
quantifiers of $\psi^{\I}$ range over saturated classes. Impredicative numerical
comprehension gives $B=\{n:n\in\UC\land\psi^{\I}(n,\ldots)\}$, and $B$ is saturated
because $\psi^{\I}$ respects $\Eqc$ in its code variable (congruence of the
interpretation: its atomic formulas respect $\Eqc$, and its class quantifiers range
over saturated classes). The definable isomorphisms are those of
\cref{hset:sf:thm:classlift}, unchanged.
\end{proof}

What stays open: $\GB$ without set Choice (\cref{hset:sf:q:gbnochoice}), and short
\emph{native} formulations of class replacement, Global Choice and $\ETR$, as
opposed to their translations (\cref{hset:sf:q:classaxioms,hset:sf:q:etr}).

As usual, a two-sorted theory can be rewritten in a one-sorted language using
disjoint tags and sort predicates. That operation does not make the class
individuals ordinary surreal numbers. It changes the logical notation while
retaining both kinds of object. The positive theorem is therefore consistent with
the obstruction in \cref{hset:sf:thm:canonicalclassnogo}. (Source~13.)

\section{Set size, cuts, and alternative readings (source 14)}
\label{hset:sf:sec:bounds}

\begin{theorem}[Birthday boundedness detects set size; source 14]\label{hset:sf:thm:bounded}
In $\GBc$, for an available class $A\subseteq\No$, the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $A$ is a set;
\item there is an ordinal $\alpha$ such that $\bd(x)<\alpha$ for every $x\in A$.
\end{enumerate}
The same assertion holds for classes of omnific integers with their native birthday
$h(x,1)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
If $A$ is a set, class replacement applied to the birthday function makes
$\{\bd(x):x\in A\}$ a set of ordinals, which has a strict ordinal bound.
Conversely,
\[
 \No_{<\alpha}=\bigcup_{\beta<\alpha}\{-,+\}^{\beta}
\]
is a set. An available class contained in this set is a set by class separation.
Restricting to the definable omnific subclass gives the second assertion.
\end{proof}

The proof uses no Choice, so the equivalence holds in $\GB$ as well; source 14
stated it in $\GBc$. Consequently, if $F$ is an available numerical class function
and $A$ is birthday-bounded, class replacement implies that $F[A]$ is
birthday-bounded. This is a direct native numerical form of a replacement
consequence. We are not claiming that this sentence by itself, over an unspecified
weak background, axiomatizes all of class replacement. That stronger equivalence
would have to account for coding choices and the existence of the relevant rank and
sign levels. (Source~14.) This report's Part~VI proves the sharp weak-base form for
definable families: over Zermelo set theory without Replacement, bounding the
birthdays of definable surreal families is equivalent to ordinal bounding, and with
a hierarchy axiom to Replacement (\cref{hset:zr:thm:equivalence,hset:zr:thm:ranked}).

\begin{corollary}[The correct scope of the surreal cut principle; source 14]\label{hset:sf:cor:cuts}
Available classes $L,R\subseteq\No$ with birthday-bounded union and $L<R$ have a
unique least-birthday separator. The assertion cannot be extended to all separated
proper-class pairs.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The boundedness hypothesis makes both sides sets by \cref{hset:sf:thm:bounded}, so
the ordinary surreal set-cut construction applies. For the failure without
boundedness, take $L=\Ord$ and $R=\varnothing$. Every surreal lies below some
ordinal, so there is no number larger than all members of $L$.
\end{proof}

This illustrates why ``every definable cut is filled'' is an unsafe axiom unless the
allowed cuts are explicitly bounded. Similarly, a proper-class family of compatible
initial sign segments need not be a single surreal number. (Source~14; the failure
is \lbl{found:prop:allcuts} of the foundations report, and Part~VI's
\cref{hset:zr:thm:setcut} is the Replacement-free set-cut theorem.)

\begin{proposition}[Proper-class branches are not additional surreals; source 14]\label{hset:sf:prop:branches}
Let $A\subseteq\Ord$ be an available class. For every ordinal $\alpha$, its
characteristic signs below $\alpha$ determine a surreal $s_\alpha$ of birthday
$\alpha$, and $s_\beta$ is a prefix of $s_\alpha$ when $\beta<\alpha$. No ordinary
surreal has all these $s_\alpha$ as prefixes.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Class separation gives $A\cap\alpha$ as a set, so the characteristic sign function
exists. Compatibility is immediate. A surreal containing all the prefixes would
have birthday at least every ordinal, which is impossible for a set-sized sign
function.
\end{proof}

Allowing such proper-class branches as objects would therefore change the
ontology. It may be an interesting new theory of generalized sign objects, but it is
not a first-order theory whose only individuals are the ordinary Conway surreal
numbers. (Source~14.)

\subsection{A larger universe can code a smaller class model}
\label{hset:sf:sub:inaccessible}

There is a genuine positive construction which should not be confused with the
same-universe proposal. Suppose the ambient metatheory has a strongly inaccessible
cardinal $\kappa$. Then
\[
 (V_\kappa,\Pow(V_\kappa))
\]
is a set-sized model of the usual class axioms, including KM comprehension and
Global Choice. All of its class objects are sets in the larger ambient universe.
Since $\card{V_\kappa}=\kappa$, an external bijection $e:\kappa\to V_\kappa$ turns
each class of that model into a sign sequence of length $\kappa$, hence into an
\emph{ambient} surreal number.

The verification of the model assertion uses inaccessibility in the usual concrete
way. Sets and their power sets stay below $\kappa$; a class function on a member of
$V_\kappa$ has range of cardinality less than $\kappa$, whose ranks are bounded by
regularity. Every external subclass needed for comprehension belongs to
$\Pow(V_\kappa)$. This is a legitimate example, not a consistency proof from $\ZFC$
alone. (Merge: Global Choice holds because a well-order of $V_\kappa$ of type $\kappa$,
induced by $e$, belongs to $\Pow(V_\kappa\times V_\kappa)$ and is coded as a class.)

The codes of length $\kappa$ do \emph{not} belong to $\No^{V_\kappa}$. Indeed the
latter has cardinality $\kappa$, whereas the full class collection of the displayed
model has cardinality $2^\kappa$. No fixed finite power or quotient of
$\No^{V_\kappa}$ can recover all those classes by an isomorphism. Using the larger
numerical universe solves a different, relative coding problem. It does not produce
a bi-interpretation of the ambient set universe with its full class universe.
(Source~14.)

\subsection{External relabelling and added predicates}

If a model and a chosen set of numerical labels have the same external cardinality,
a bijection can transport its relations to those labels. This works for essentially
any carrier, not specifically for surreal numbers. It need not preserve native
addition, multiplication, birthday, or internal definability. In the proper-class
setting the existence of a suitable higher-level bijection is itself an additional
assumption.

A proposed relation $R$ on numbers can also be postulated to have the entire
strength of set or class membership. Unless its numerical meaning and the inverse
interpretation are given, this is a relabelling of the desired foundation. The
constructions in this article avoid that by using a concrete birthday operation,
explicit graph formulas, and proved round trips. The NBG obstruction explains why
that standard cannot simply be extended to all class objects in the same way.
(Source~14.)

\subsection{Second-order numerical logic}

One may phrase \cref{hset:sf:thm:classbi} as a theory with numerical individual
variables and second-order variables for classes of numbers. Under Henkin semantics
this is a notational variant of the two-sorted theory already constructed. In that
sense it is a perfectly sound ``surreal-based NBG.'' It is not a first-order theory
with only numerical objects, since its second-order variables retain the class
collection.

Replacing Henkin semantics by full second-order semantics is another substantial
change. It requires all external subclasses of the numerical domain and no longer
represents arbitrary NBG class realizations. Accordingly full semantics must not be
used to claim the first-order bi-interpretability established here. (Source~14.)

\section{Formalization programmes and finite checks}
\label{hset:sf:sec:formal}

\subsection{What is, and is not, machine verified}
\label{hset:sf:sub:machine}

The repository's formalization ledger explicitly distinguishes written statements,
mathematical review, and completed Lean declarations. That distinction is preserved
here \cite{ProveItSF13}. The module
\path{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/Surreal/Foundations/OmnificSupportBounds.lean} contains
the following declarations in the namespace
\path{Surreal.Foundations.SignSequence}:
\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{omnific\_monomial\_clearing};
\item \texttt{surreal\_eq\_omnific\_fraction};
\item \texttt{omnificSurrealIsFractionRing}.
\end{itemize}
The sign-sequence infrastructure also supplies birthdays, ordinal embeddings, signs,
and prefixes. At the inspected commit, the birthday-cutoff report stated that none
of its \texttt{hset:} labels had a Lean implementation mapping. (Source~13; both
statements are true at the placement, and the three declarations are mapped to the
fraction-field instance in \path{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/docs/FORMALIZATION.md} as
proved. That covers \cref{hset:sf:prop:fractions} only; nothing else in this part
is formalized.)

This article does not report a fresh build of those targets. More importantly, the
explicit native interpretation and its inverse-comparison theorem have not been
supplied as new Lean proofs in this research task. A compiled PDF is not a
mathematical proof-assistant verification.

A practical mechanization should proceed in the following dependency order.
(Source~13.)
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Prove the native ordinal and sign definitions.} Formalize $\Ord$,
$\EqB$, $\Prefp$, $\Plusp$ and their equivalence to the canonical semantic notions,
over both $\No$ and $\Oz$. The omnific initiality witness in the prefix proof is a
key obligation.
\item \textbf{Formalize the block function.} Keep natural and ordinary ordinal
operations as distinct typed operations. Prove disjointness and limit endpoints
before constructing the sign word. Include the negative filling of gaps and all
unused pairing indices.
\item \textbf{Formalize bounded relation codes.} Prove the pairing lemma,
graph-of-function clauses, and the unary finite-tuple injection. The actual vertex
ordinal must remain distinct from the larger matrix base.
\item \textbf{Connect graph validity to collapse.} Use the coverage theorem for all
bounded subsets to identify the first-order well-foundedness test with
set-theoretic well-foundedness. Prove root accessibility and the equality and
membership comparison lemmas. The membership embedding must reflect as well as
preserve edges.
\item \textbf{Prove both comparison maps.} Formalize the ordinal graph relation
$\OG$, then $\Cmp_{\Dd}$, and prove sign-function equality. Verify raw injectivity,
surjectivity onto every reconstructed number, and preservation of all language
symbols.
\item \textbf{Formalize syntax and axiom translation.} Represent the finite
numerical language, quotient interpretations, recursive axiom schemes, and
$\operatorname{Iso}(\Cmp_{\Dd})$. A semantic construction inside one ambient type
universe should not be mistaken for an internal interpretation in every model.
\item \textbf{Lift to class theories separately.} Formalize saturated numerical
classes, both class comparison maps, and translated class replacement. Keep the
model-cardinality obstruction in the metatheory; it is not an internal statement
that a model's proper class has an external cardinality.
\end{enumerate}
The first five steps establish the mathematical construction. The sixth verifies the
precise first-order bi-interpretability claim. Combining them is preferable to
treating informal code representations as if they already constituted an
interpretation.

\subsection{A universe-safe implementation plan (source 14)}

A proof-assistant development should separate three levels. First, define sign
sequences whose ordinal domains are small in a fixed universe, and prove the prefix,
bit, and polynomial-pairing lemmas. Second, define rooted graphs, the code
equivalence relation, and the membership relation on the quotient. Third, define
first-order syntax, interpretation composition, and the explicit round-trip maps.

For a Lean-style implementation, a type of surreals small in universe $u$ generally
lives in a larger universe; it must not be asserted to be an element of itself. A
quotient of codes is a quotient type in the appropriate metatheoretic universe, not
a new set in the represented object theory. Similarly, a class collection is
additional structure; using the full ambient type of predicates as the class sort
would silently impose stronger external comprehension than an arbitrary Henkin model
of GB possesses.

The central implementation milestones are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{F\arabic*.}]
\item Formalize \eqref{hset:sf:eq:prefix}, \eqref{hset:sf:eq:bit}, and
\eqref{hset:sf:eq:pair}, including ordinal discreteness under natural arithmetic.
\item Prove the well-founded extensional collapse interface and the root-hull
membership theorem without choosing global representatives.
\item Implement interpretation syntax and prove the generic \cref{hset:sf:lem:rt},
including finite-dimensional quotient flattening.
\item Formalize the number/sign-function relation $\rho$, including operation
preservation, then derive $\SArt$.
\item Formalize fraction equivalence and quotient birthday, and prove O4 is exactly
the required omnific recovery condition.
\item Implement the saturated-class lift and separately formalize the reflection and
diagonal obstructions in suitable metatheories.
\end{enumerate}
This report does not supply Lean proofs for these milestones. Existing repository
code may support individual ingredients, but a matching filename or theorem label is
not evidence of a completed formalization of the interpretation theorem.

\subsection{Executable finite checks (source 14)}
\label{hset:sf:sub:checks}

The accompanying program, shipped as
\path{code/14-surreal-only-nbg-verify_finite.py}, uses only the Python standard
library. It checks the prefix formula on a finite sign tree, the pairing on finite
ordinals and on a finite family of Cantor-normal-form polynomials, padding and
decoding of finite graph codes, equality and membership by graph isomorphism, finite
tuple packing, dyadic quotient-birthday invariance, and the finite diagonal
identity. Its record, shipped as \path{data/14-surreal-only-nbg-verification.json},
records the actual counts.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lr@{}}
\toprule
Finite check & Cases verified\\
\midrule
Prefix pairs & 16,129\\
Finite / polynomial ordinal pairs & 6,400 / 4,096\\
Labelled valid rooted graphs & 207\\
Graph equality / membership comparisons & 42,849 / 42,849\\
Tuple-packing round trips & 961\\
Dyadic fraction-invariance checks & 1,225\\
Boolean matrices checked for the diagonal identity & 66,066\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

These tests are designed to detect indexing, orientation, padding, normalization,
and quotient mistakes. They do not establish transfinite well-foundedness, ZFC
consistency, interpretation of an infinite axiom scheme, or the class-theoretic
theorems. The displayed mathematical proofs, not a finite test run, carry those
claims. (Source~14.)

\noindent[Added 4 October 2026, batch~95: the record also lists 642 sign-bit
checks, 75 increasing-DAG candidates and 511 dyadic sign-birthday checks, which the
table omits. For this merge the shipped program was rerun with Python 3.14.4 on a
copy outside the repository: it passed in about two seconds and reproduced the
shipped record exactly, apart from line endings (CRLF on Windows). Without an
argument it writes \path{verification.json} next to itself, so it must be run on a
copy; the report's README gives the command. Source 13 ships no program.]

\section{Further questions and research}
\label{hset:sf:sec:research}

The questions below are proposed research directions. Unless a published source is
explicitly named, they are not claimed to be previously published open problems, and
no assertion of exhaustive novelty is made. Several are simplification or
classification problems rather than existence problems. (Source~13.) Source 14's
questions are tasks left unresolved by that report; they are not all asserted to be
previously published open problems, no claim of absence from the literature is
made, and each is stated so that a future result can be checked against a definite
language or base theory. Questions asked by both sources are printed once, with
both credited. The last subsection lists, under the standing rule on unproved
claims, the inputs both sources import without proof.

\subsection{Intrinsic axioms and minimum structure}

\begin{question}[Short intrinsic omnific axioms; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:shortaxioms}
Can one replace the translated $\ZFC$ schemes and the full comparison axioms by a
transparent list of arithmetic, initiality, generation, and birthday-bound principles
in the native language?
\end{question}

$\Tth_{\Oz}$ settles the recursive-axiomatization existence problem. It leaves open
the most attractive presentation of that theory. A candidate shorter system must
prove that every original number is recovered; proving only that graph codes form
some model of set theory does not suffice. Assuming consistency, a finite
axiomatization in a finite language is ruled out by bi-interpretability, so the
realistic target is a small collection of natural schemes.

\begin{question}[Intrinsic axioms versus round-trip axioms; source 14]\label{hset:sf:q:intrinsic}
Can one prove, in a fully checked first-order development, that a natural list of
order, birthday, recursion, bounded-cut, replacement, and power-set principles is
equivalent to $\SArt$? The target is to eliminate the explicit coherence axioms of
\eqref{hset:sf:eq:rtaxioms} by proving them from intrinsic numerical principles. A
useful deliverable would be a literal axiom list and a formal derivation of both
round trips, not only their verification in the intended class structure.
\end{question}

Source 14 closes its list thus: the most direct next mathematical target is
\cref{hset:sf:q:unary}, which asks whether the explicit positive omnific construction
can be made much more intrinsic; the most valuable foundational verification target
is \cref{hset:sf:q:intrinsic}; both have a concrete success criterion, and neither
requires claiming a breakthrough before a proof is checked.

Both questions contain the unverified part of the Chen--Hamkins--Yang programme: its
candidate intrinsic axioms for $\SAth$ (\cref{hset:sf:sub:imported}), and they meet
the first item of \cref{hset:q:axiomatics}.

\begin{question}[Reducts of the birthday language; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:reducts}
Which proper reducts of $\{0,1,+,-,\cdot,<,\precb\}$ still support a canonical
bi-interpretation with $\ZFC$? In particular, can multiplication be eliminated from
the native omnific construction?
\end{question}

The current proof uses multiplication to address blocks and matrix entries.
Recovering that addressing mechanism in a reduct would be a concrete first step.
Conversely, a suitable family of automorphisms or a model-theoretic tameness result
could prove that a proposed reduct is insufficient. Merely replacing birthday
precedence by the definable birthday function is a change of presentation, not a new
open question. (Source~13.) [Merge: by \cref{hset:sf:prop:reducts}, for comparisons
sending each number to codes of itself the question asks which reducts define the
remaining symbols of $\Lb$; it overlaps the ``Minimal signature'' question of
\cref{hset:sec:questions}, which asks for one-way interpretations, and Question~42.3 of
\repo{definable-surreals-and-omnific-integers}, ``How much of the signature is
necessary?'', for $\mathfrak S_\Omega$.]

\begin{question}[Pure omnific interpretation strength; sources 13, 14]\label{hset:sf:q:pureoz}
What is the exact first-order interpretation strength of the pure ordered omnific
ring? Can it interpret a set theory through a genuinely different, noncanonical
reconstruction? (Source 13.) Does the pure ordered omnific ring interpret the set
universe, with a uniform definable reverse reconstruction, or can such an
interpretation be ruled out? This is not the question of recursive axiomatizability
of its complete theory, and the real-closed-field decidability argument does not
apply to this ring. (Source 14.)
\end{question}

\Cref{hset:sf:cor:nondefbirthday} excludes recovering the canonical birthday
predicate by a pure-ring formula. The automorphism argument also excludes the stated
canonical bi-interpretation with the actual well-founded $V$
(\cref{hset:sf:prop:purenogo}). Neither statement resolves the broader
interpretation question. Work on this problem should keep those three claims
separate. (Source~13.) [Merge: answered in part. The pure ring interprets
$(\N,+,\cdot)$ and the field $\No$ with $\R$ (\cref{hset:sf:rem:pureoz}), so its
strength is at least arithmetic; a reconstruction with the canonical set-to-number
direction is excluded by \cref{hset:sf:prop:purenogo}; whether it interprets $\ZF$
or $\ZFC$ at all, by a noncanonical reconstruction, is open.]

\begin{question}[Short fractional-birthday definitions; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:shortFB}
Find a direct, reasonably short formula defining $\FB(a,b,c,d)$ in the native omnific
birthday structure, and compare its quantifier complexity with the
interpretation-based definition.
\end{question}

Existence is already proved in \cref{hset:sf:cor:fractionbirthday}. A substantially
shorter definition could make fraction-field transfers practical and reveal
arithmetic constraints on birthdays that the graph construction hides.

\begin{question}[Unary birthday on omnific integers; source 14]\label{hset:sf:q:unary}
Is the quotient-birthday operation $h(a,b)$ definable in
\[
 (\Oz;0,1,+,-,\cdot,<,\bd\restr\Oz)?
\]
A positive answer would remove the binary enrichment from
\cref{hset:sf:thm:omnifictheory}. A negative answer should produce models or
symmetries agreeing on the weaker structure but disagreeing on a quotient birthday.
Neither the field fraction construction alone nor knowledge of the two input
birthdays settles this.
\end{question}

\noindent[Added 4 October 2026, batch~95: answered positively by source 13's
\cref{hset:sf:cor:fractionbirthday}, through \cref{hset:sf:prop:unary}; the
definition is interpretation-based and long, and \cref{hset:sf:q:shortFB} asks for a
short one. Source 14 called this ``the most direct next mathematical target''.]

\begin{question}[Quantifier complexity of the omnific predicate; source 14]\label{hset:sf:q:omnificpred}
What is the least first-order complexity of $\operatorname{Omnific}(x)$ in $\Ls$? The
definition obtained through normal forms and set codes is effective but expensive.
An economical birthday-and-order characterization could substantially simplify O4 and
subsequent formalization.
\end{question}

The omnific sign criterion \ref{hset:sf:item:signcrit} of \cref{hset:sf:sec:bits},
if read with $\Plusp$, gives a candidate: $x\in\Oz$ iff no ordinal $\gamma$ with
$\gamma+1<\bd(x)$ has $\Plusp(x,\gamma)\not\leftrightarrow\Plusp(x,\gamma+1)$. This
merge records it as a candidate only: it is a short $\Ls$-formula whose correctness
is the imported criterion, and its exact complexity is not computed here.

\subsection{Weaker foundations and bounded universes}

\begin{question}[Choice-free numerical foundations; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:choicefree}
Is there a canonical numerical bi-interpretation for $\ZF$ without Choice using a
different decoder? If so, what replaces ordinal-labeled transitive closures?
\end{question}

The present decoder has exact range $\HWO$. This is a proved limitation of that
decoder, not an impossibility theorem for every conceivable one. Any proposed
replacement must explain how it represents sets whose transitive closures are not
well-orderable, while keeping each individual code a standard set-sized number.

\begin{question}[Removing Choice; source 14]\label{hset:sf:q:removechoice}
Which choice-free universes can be recovered by ordinal-sign graph codes? The proof
of \cref{hset:sf:lem:collapse} uses a well-ordering of each transitive closure. In ZF
this may fail. Determine the exact class of sets recovered by the same
interpretation, and whether a modified numerical language removes the obstruction
without quietly adding a well-ordering principle.
\end{question}

\noindent[Added 4 October 2026, batch~95: the first part is answered, and was
already answered at source 14's pin: the recovered sets are exactly
$\HWO$ (\cref{hset:thm:choice}, source 06's, and \cref{hset:sf:prop:hwo}). The
modified-language part is \cref{hset:sf:q:choicefree}, still open; this report's
\cref{hset:rem:answered-choice} asks the same.]

\begin{question}[Birthday cutoffs; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:cutoffs}
For which ordinals or cardinals $\kappa$ does the native structure on
$\Oz_{<\kappa}=\{x\in\Oz:\bd(x)<\kappa\}$ recover a natural hereditary set universe?
What closure hypotheses are exact?
\end{question}

The repository already studies related surreal cutoffs \cite{ProveItSF13}. The
omnific codec adds explicit bounds involving $\alpha\mapsto\omega\alpha+\omega$ and
$\kappa\mapsto\kappa^2$, all naturally computed. Limit discontinuities must be
included in any sharp estimate. One must also check whether the chosen cutoff is
closed under the arithmetic symbols of the proposed structure. (Source~13.) [Merge:
for $\No_{<\lambda}$ this is Parts~I--III of this report (\cref{hset:part:spine});
the omnific cutoffs are not treated there.]

\begin{question}[Fragments and axiom strength; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:fragments}
Which numerical schemes correspond exactly to Separation, Replacement, Power Set,
and weaker collection principles? Can natural fragments of omnific arithmetic
recover admissible or other restricted set universes?
\end{question}

A useful outcome would be a table of equivalences with proofs in both directions,
not just upper bounds obtained by translating all of set theory. The finite PA/HF
correspondence suggests an approach through induction and coding closure, but
transfinite well-foundedness and Choice introduce additional issues. (Source~13.)
[Merge: for the surreal field this report has partial answers: eternity is
Replacement at cardinal cutoffs and the translated Power Set sentence detects strong
limits (Part~II), and over Zermelo set theory definable birthday bounding is ordinal
bounding (\cref{hset:zr:thm:equivalence,hset:zr:thm:ranked}).]

\begin{question}[Selecting codes and choice principles; sources 13, 14]\label{hset:sf:q:selection}
What extra principle is equivalent to a definable selection of a graph code for every
represented set? What weaker selections can be made on bounded families?
(Source 13.) Under what additional definable choice principles can the quotient
$\Code/{\Eqc}$ be replaced by a definable subclass of unique numerical
representatives? Determine whether the resulting presentation is
\emph{definitionally equivalent} to the associated set theory, rather than merely
bi-interpretable. Adding an arbitrary Global Choice class as a parameter changes the
theory and must be recorded. (Source 14, ``Canonical representatives and
definitional equivalence''.)
\end{question}

The main construction needs existence of some code and deliberately avoids selecting
one globally. A selected ordinal labeling of each transitive closure gives a way to
choose a least-labeled member of each nonempty set. Conversely, a suitable Global
Choice class can be used to choose labelings. A precise analysis should distinguish
class parameters, parameter-free definability, and bounded selection schemes.
(Source~13.)

\begin{question}[Class theory without set Choice; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:gbnochoice}
Is there a numerical class lift bi-interpretable with $\GB$ without set Choice? The
present ordinal-graph codec uses set Choice (\cref{hset:sf:prop:hwo}); $\GB$ without
it ``needs a different analysis''.
\end{question}

\subsection{Class theories, models, and proof complexity}

\begin{question}[Native class axioms; sources 13, 14]\label{hset:sf:q:classaxioms}
Find compact numerical formulations of class replacement, Global Choice, and
elementary transfinite recursion, and prove their exact equivalence to the translated
principles in $\Tth^{\mathrm{cl}}_{\Dd}$. (Source 13.) Over a carefully specified
weak numerical two-sorted base, characterize which birthday-boundedness and packing
principles are equivalent to class replacement, elementary comprehension, and Power
Set. The implication proved in \cref{hset:sf:sec:bounds} is not yet such a reverse
mathematics result. (Source 14, ``Minimal native class axioms''.)
\end{question}

The two-sorted existence theorem is established. The remaining task is to expose the
mathematical content of the class principles in numerical terms. Strengthening
comprehension changes the available classes, so $\GBC$, its recursion extensions, and
$\KM$ should be analyzed separately rather than grouped under one unspecified class
theory. (Source~13.) [Merge: the translated forms are bi-interpretable with their
sources by \cref{hset:sf:cor:variants}; the question asks for native forms.]

\begin{question}[Elementary transfinite recursion; source 14]\label{hset:sf:q:etr}
Formulate a native numerical version of $\ETR$, and prove its exact equivalence to the
class-theoretic principle under \cref{hset:sf:thm:classbi}. Distinguish set-sized
recursion, already used in surreal construction, from recursion with class-sized
stages or class values. The latter must not be silently assumed in a GB proof.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Class realizations and forcing; source 14]\label{hset:sf:q:forcing}
Use the inclusion-preserving correspondence \eqref{hset:sf:eq:inclusion} to transfer
concrete realization and class-forcing questions to surreal-based GBC. Which
numerical predicates correspond to adjoining a new amenable class without adding
sets? This could make precise what information remains invisible to the underlying
birthday field.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Nonstandard models in numerical terms; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:nonstandard}
How can familiar invariants of set-theoretic models be recognized intrinsically in
models of $\Tth_{\Oz}$? What do nonstandard ordinals, ill-founded external
membership, and different class realizations look like in the numerical
presentation?
\end{question}

Bi-interpretability already transports the relevant model-theoretic information and
definable comparisons. The research task is a useful intrinsic description, not
another existence proof. In particular, rigidity of the actual numerical universe
must not be extrapolated to all countable or nonstandard models. (Source~13; it meets
the item ``ill-founded models'' of \cref{hset:q:axiomatics}.)

\begin{question}[Generalized sign objects; source 14]\label{hset:sf:q:signobjects}
Can a theory of proper-class sign branches be axiomatized so that it extends, rather
than contradicts, ordinary surreal arithmetic, while admitting a meaningful partial
arithmetic? Specify its ambient higher-order foundation before discussing a universe
of all such branches. It is not enough to write a Conway cut with a proper-class
option set and assume a number exists.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Effective proof translations and formal proof; sources 13, 14]\label{hset:sf:q:formal}
What are the formula-length and proof-length costs of the explicit interpretations?
Can graph, ordinal, and function coding be modularized without concealing hypotheses?
(Source 13.) Construct a proof-assistant implementation of the code interface,
including actual first-order formula syntax, and measure the cost of translating
natural set-theoretic statements. Can common set operations be compiled into
substantially smaller formulas than the generic normal-form and graph-code route
produces? (Source 14, ``Formal proof and proof complexity''.)
\end{question}

The translations are effective, but effectiveness does not imply low complexity.
Fixed abbreviations, definitional extensions, and a reusable syntactic library could
reduce formalization overhead. None of these improvements would make the resulting
theory decidable. (Source~13.)

\begin{question}[Automorphisms of richer arithmetic; source 13]\label{hset:sf:q:kks}
How do the native birthday results interact with the published automorphism
questions for surreal exponentiation and Conway's monomial map?
\end{question}

Kaplan, Krapp, and Serra pose questions about nontrivial exponential
$1$-automorphisms, automorphisms preserving the monomial map, and extensions of order
automorphisms of generalized epsilon numbers \cite[Questions 5.4, 5.6, 5.7]{KKS}. The
shear automorphisms used here need not preserve those extra operations, so they do
not answer those questions. Comparing the exact published hypotheses with the
repository's constructions is a worthwhile next step; the present article makes no
claim to have resolved them. (Source~13.)

\subsection{Inputs imported without proof}
\label{hset:sf:sub:imported}

Under the standing rule on unproved claims, the following inputs, used by sources 13
and 14 and kept by this merge, are recorded as imported. Each is a published or
announced result, credited at its point of use; none was re-proved here, and each
item says what is missing for a self-contained account in the collection.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item \emph{Omnific sign criterion and initiality} (\ref{hset:sf:item:initial},
\ref{hset:sf:item:signcrit} of \cref{hset:sf:sec:bits}; Ehrlich--Kaplan arXiv~v1
\S2.2, Lemma~2.1 and the proof of Lemma~2.3, after Gonshor, Theorem~8.1). Every native
$\Oz$ result of this part depends on it: \cref{hset:sf:lem:prefix} in $\Oz$,
\cref{hset:sf:thm:bitstorage}, \cref{hset:sf:thm:roundtrip} and
\cref{hset:sf:thm:theories} for $\Oz$, \cref{hset:sf:cor:fractionbirthday} and
\cref{hset:sf:prop:unary}. Source 14's quotient-birthday route does not. Missing: a
proof in the collection, or a formalization against the sign-sequence library;
neither the Hilbert's-tenth intake nor this merge checked the cited proofs.
\item \emph{Hahn lifting of exponent automorphisms} (the step ``an additive order
automorphism of the exponent group \dots{} is an ordered-field automorphism'' in
\cref{hset:sf:prop:automorphism}; Kaplan--Krapp--Serra \cite{KKS}). Missing: a
written proof that the lifted map is onto $\No$ and multiplicative on Conway normal
forms; the collection's \repo{surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms} uses such
lifts for its stronger \lbl{opa:thm:parameters}.
\item \emph{Class-forcing facts} for \cref{hset:sf:lem:twosizes}: a countable
$\GBC$-realization of a countable $M$ has a proper countable extension with the same
sets (a class Cohen generic), and unions of increasing chains of
$\GBC$-realizations of one $M$ are realizations (Williams \cite{WilliamsDiss},
Theorem~1.61(5), the proof of Theorem~1.65). Source 13 checks the union step for
comprehension, replacement and Global Choice; the extension step is imported.
\item \emph{Finite axiomatizability of $\GBC$/$\NBG$} (\cref{hset:sf:thm:noint};
Williams, Corollary~3.11 and its proof), and the L\'evy reflection theorem and
G\"odel's second incompleteness theorem \cite{Levy,Godel}: classical.
\item \emph{Attribution of the reflection argument to Hamkins--Yao, Section~4}: checked
for this merge and correct (\cref{hset:sf:thm:noint}).
\item \emph{Je\v{r}\'abek's discussion} of omnific axiomatization and of the complete
theory of $\Oz$ \cite{JerabekOz}, cited by source 14 for $\Frac(\Oz)=\No$ (proved
here) and for the non-recursive axiomatizability of the complete theory (not used in
any proof).
\item \emph{The Chen--Hamkins--Yang intrinsic axioms} for $\SAth$: announced, with the
slides marking details as still being checked; neither source verifies them, and
\cref{hset:sf:thm:theories,hset:sf:thm:surrealtheory} do not depend on them. This is
\cref{hset:sf:q:intrinsic}.
\item \emph{That $(V_\kappa,\Pow(V_\kappa))$ satisfies $\KM$ with Global Choice for
inaccessible $\kappa$} (\cref{hset:sf:sub:inaccessible}): standard; source 14
sketches the verification.
\end{enumerate}
No claim of either source was found to be wrong. The KM, $\GBc$ and $\ETR$ class
lifts that both sources sketched are proved in \cref{hset:sf:cor:variants}; source
13's paragraph on the pure omnific ring is \cref{hset:sf:prop:purenogo}.

\section{Conclusions of sources 13 and 14, audit and provenance}
\label{hset:sf:sec:provenance}

\subsection{Final assessment (source 13)}

The finite-set analogy extends fully to the set universe once the numerical language
retains constructional information. Arithmetic, order, and birthday precedence allow
an omnific integer to store arbitrary bounded ordinal data. Well-founded extensional
graph codes reconstruct sets; signwise comparison reconstructs the original numbers.
The explicit recursive axiom construction turns these facts into
\[
 \Tth_{\No}\bi\ZFC\bi\Tth_{\Oz}.
\]

The corresponding statement for full class theory needs a different ontology. A
uniform interpretation whose numerical individuals are all original sets cannot
recover every possible class part of a $\GBC$-model. Retaining classes of numbers
gives a faithful two-sorted theory, with explicit comparison maps and no global
selection of code representatives.

The constructive omnific result and the class boundary are the most useful outcomes
of this investigation. They clarify what has been proved, which language features
are doing the work, and which further questions concern genuine simplification or
new foundational strength. They do not settle an independent set-theoretic statement
or establish a literature-priority claim.

\subsection{Conclusions and limits of the result (source 14)}

There is a rigorous and natural set-level realization of the proposed analogy.
Ordinal-length signs replace finite bits; birthday makes the sign information
first-order accessible; well-founded graph collapse recovers arbitrary sets; and
explicit coherence supplies the second round trip. An omnific-integer implementation
is available through fraction reconstruction and the quotient-birthday operation.

The resulting theories are $\ZFC$-equivalent, not NBG-equivalent. For uniform
theories interpretable in $\ZFC$, reflection and incompleteness forbid even a one-way
interpretation of the finitely axiomatized NBG theory. For native same-universe class
names with elementary evaluation, the diagonal argument independently forbids
assigning an ordinary set-sized numerical code to every class.

The correct NBG-level construction is reversible but retains a class sort. It
replaces sets by numerical graph codes and classes by saturated classes of codes, and
it recovers arbitrary numerical subclasses in the second round trip. The distinction
is substantive: the class realization is preserved rather than discarded.

The report therefore answers the formulation problem with an explicit positive
construction and a precisely delimited negative theorem. It does not claim to
originate the Chen--Hamkins--Yang bi-interpretation, to settle the pure omnific-ring
problem, to certify a minimal intrinsic SA axiom system, or to prove consistency of
any of these foundations. Its proposed follow-on results have complete mathematical
arguments here, but their novelty has not been independently certified and their
proofs have not been checked in a proof assistant.

\subsection{Formula expansion and type checking}
\label{hset:sf:sub:formulas}

\paragraph{Quantifier and domain conventions (source 13).} In numerical formulas,
every unqualified variable ranges over $\Dd$. The abbreviation
$\forall i<\nu\,\varphi$ means $\forall i\,((\Ord(i)\land i<\nu)\to\varphi)$.
Existential bounded ordinal quantifiers use conjunction instead of implication. The
relation $x\le y$ is $x<y\lor x=y$. A definable function such as $\bd(x)$ can be
eliminated by introducing a variable and its unique defining relation.

A code variable $c$ stands for three numerical variables $(\nu_c,r_c,z_c)$. Both
$\Eqc(c,d)$ and $\Memc(c,d)$ include $\Valid(c)\land\Valid(d)$ as conjuncts. Thus
they are false on invalid arguments. No equality of quotient objects is substituted
for raw numerical equality inside the definitions of the codes.

For a candidate $h$ for a map from $\nu_c$ to $\nu_d$, the required clauses are:
\[
\begin{split}
 \operatorname{Total}(h)&:\quad \forall i<\nu_c\,\exists j<\nu_d\,Y_h(i,j),\\
 \operatorname{Functional}(h)&:\quad \forall i<\nu_c\,\forall j,k<\nu_d\,((Y_h(i,j)\land Y_h(i,k))\to j=k),\\
 \operatorname{Injective}(h)&:\quad \forall i,k<\nu_c\,\forall j<\nu_d\,((Y_h(i,j)\land Y_h(k,j))\to i=k),\\
 \operatorname{Surjective}(h)&:\quad \forall j<\nu_d\,\exists i<\nu_c\,Y_h(i,j).
\end{split}
\]
Edge preservation and reflection are the biconditional in \cref{hset:sf:sec:graphs}.
Equality of set codes uses all four displayed conditions and root preservation.
Membership uses the first three, edge preservation and reflection, predecessor
closure, and the root-edge condition in \eqref{hset:sf:eq:mem}. These formulas use a
fixed number of variables regardless of the ordinal sizes of the graphs. Their
quantification over map codes $h$ is still first-order quantification over numbers.

\paragraph{The second comparison as one formula (source 13).} Suppressing only the
fixed triple expansion, the formula $\Cmp_{\Dd}(x,c)$ can be written
\[
\begin{split}
 \Valid(c)\land \operatorname{Is}_{\Dd}^{\I_{\Dd}}(c)\land
 \exists\lambda\bigl[&\Ord(\lambda)\land\EqB(x,\lambda)\\
 {}\land{}&\exists e\,(\OG(\lambda,e)\land\operatorname{Dom}^{\I_{\Dd}}(c,e))\\
 {}\land{}&\forall\beta<\lambda\,\forall e\,\bigl(\OG(\beta,e)\to
 (\operatorname{Val}_+^{\I_{\Dd}}(c,e)\leftrightarrow\Plusp(x,\beta))\bigr)\bigr].
\end{split}
\]
The source predicates $\operatorname{Dom},\operatorname{Val}_+,\operatorname{Is}_{\Dd}$
refer to the fixed sign-function representation. Their translations are effective
substitutions. They do not name a satisfaction relation.

\paragraph{Formula-expansion and type-checking checklist (source 14).} A literal
compiler for the definitions of source 14 can follow the following fixed rules. They
make precise why mathematical abbreviations do not add a hidden sort of sets to
$\SArt$. Replace $\Ord(x)$ by $\bd(x)=x$. Replace $\alpha<\delta$ in an ordinal-bound
quantifier by the conjunction of this formula with the order inequality. Replace
$\Prefp$, $\Bit$, $\varpi$, and $\Lambda_{14}$ by \eqref{hset:sf:eq:prefix},
\eqref{hset:sf:eq:bit}, and the indicated polynomials. Replace every variable for a
subset of $\delta$ by one numerical variable constrained to have birthday $\delta$.
Replace every rectangular relation variable by one numerical variable of birthday
$\Lambda_{14}(\delta+\eta)$, with its membership predicate read at $\varpi(i,j)$.
Extra unused bits in witnesses can either be ignored or fixed to minus; the two
versions define the same existence claims.

A vertex map from $\delta$ onto a definable subset $H\subseteq\eta$ is a rectangular
relation $R$ subject to
\begin{align*}
 &\forall i<\delta\,\exists!j<\eta\,[H(j)\land R(i,j)],\\
 &\forall j<\eta\,[H(j)\to\exists!i<\delta\,R(i,j)],\\
 &\forall i<\delta\,\forall j<\eta\,[R(i,j)\to H(j)].
\end{align*}
Preservation of edges is
\[
 R(i,j)\land R(i',j')\quad\longrightarrow\quad(iE_ci'\leftrightarrow jE_dj').
\]
Preservation of the root adds $R(r_c,r_d)$, or $R(r_c,j_0)$ for the hull rooted at
$j_0$. These conditions provide the full expanded content of the isomorphism
abbreviations used in \cref{hset:sf:sec:graphs}. (Here $H$ is a definable vertex set,
not a hereditary universe.)

For a set formula, translate atomic equality by $\Eqc$, membership by $\Memc$,
Boolean connectives homomorphically, and each quantifier by a quantifier over three
numerical coordinates restricted by $\Code$. Capture-free substitution uses renamed
bound variables. A composite interpretation substitutes these finite formulas again;
its tuple dimension stays finite. Isomorphism preservation for a function symbol is a
formula about its graph, so no interpretation of partial terms is required.

The following distinctions should be explicit in an implementation: $0,1$ as surreal
ring constants; $0,1$ as interpreted set integers; an ordinal as an all-plus number;
the code $c^{\mathrm{ord}}_\alpha$ for that ordinal as a set; the original number
$x$; and a set code $c$ for its sign function in $\rho(x,c)$. Conflating any pair of
these is a common source of false round-trip proofs.

\subsection{Non-claims of sources 13 and 14}
\label{hset:sf:sub:nonclaims}

Every limitation stated by either source, in its manuscript, its README or its
source-and-status note, is kept; most appear at their point of use. Collected.

\paragraph{Source 13.} The Chen--Hamkins--Yang result is not presented as new, and no
unpublished final axiomatization is assumed verified; $\Tth_{\Dd}$ is not claimed
complete, categorical or finitely axiomatized; no literature priority is asserted,
no previously published open problem is claimed solved, and novelty is an audit
result about the inspected material only; no proof assistant was used, no new theorem
is claimed machine-checked, no fresh Lean build was made, and a compiled PDF is not a
verification; $\HWO$ is the limit of this decoder only, not an impossibility theorem
for every interpretation of $\ZF$, and $\HWO$ need not model $\ZF$; no global code
selection is made; definability transfer is per formula, supplies no truth
predicate, and excludes class-quantified formulas; the pure-$\Oz$ result does not
exclude every (noncanonical) interpretation of set theory; rigidity concerns the
actual universe, not all models of $\Tth_{\Dd}$; the class no-go does not forbid
external relabelling of particular countable models or one-sorted presentations
with class individuals; the $\GB$ diagonal does not cover formulas with bound class
quantifiers; the class lift keeps a class sort; $\GB$ without set Choice is open;
the shear automorphisms do not answer Kaplan--Krapp--Serra's Questions 5.4, 5.6 and
5.7; nothing independent of $\ZFC$ is decided; the main constructions received
``independent proof reviews during preparation'', which are distinct from external
peer review or formalization.

\paragraph{Source 14.} The CHY bi-interpretation is not this report's; no priority
is claimed for its deductions; nothing is Lean-verified and no Lean development or
fully serialized first-order syntax compiler is included; the finite checks are
supplementary and do not verify transfinite interpretations, infinite schemes, class
comprehension, reflection or consistency; the negative theorem needs ``uniformly
interpretable in $\ZFC$'' and excludes neither external relabelling, added predicates
of independent strength, nor a larger universe; $\SArt$ does not verify CHY's
candidate axioms; $\OAqb$ is not the complete theory of $\Oz$ and does not evade
Je\v{r}\'abek's obstruction; $h$ is not claimed definable in the pure ring; the
pure-field argument is not a no-go theorem for the pure omnific ring; the reflection
argument is classical and not a new separation of ZFC and NBG; the
finite-axiomatizability diagnostic alone does not exclude a one-way interpretation;
the diagonal concerns internally available evaluators; full second-order semantics
is not claimed; \cref{hset:sf:thm:bounded} is not a reverse-mathematics
axiomatization; the inaccessible example is not a same-universe solution nor a
consistency proof from $\ZFC$; the repository audit was targeted, with no
nonduplication or novelty guarantee, and it did not build or audit this report or
import its cutoff claims as axioms.

the imported omnific sign criterion; \cref{hset:sf:prop:reducts} does not decide

\appendix
\section{Formula expansion and size checks}
\label{hset:app:formulas}

\subsection{What ``first-order'' means in the coding proof}

Every abbreviation expands to a finite formula in
$\{0,1,+,-,\cdot,<,\bd\}$. Bounded ordinal quantifiers are shorthand for
ordinary scalar quantifiers with guards $\Ord(x)$ and $x<\delta$. A set variable
in an interpreted membership formula becomes a triple of scalar variables.
Equality becomes $\sim$, membership becomes $\in_\I$, and quantifiers are
relativized to $\Code$:
\[
 (u=v)^\I=(C_u\sim C_v),\quad (u\in v)^\I=(C_u\in_\I C_v),\quad
 (\exists u\,\varphi)^\I=\exists\delta_u\exists r_u\exists t_u\,
 (\Code(C_u)\land\varphi^\I),
\]
commuting with the connectives. Induction on formulas proves
$H_{\kappa(\lambda)}\models\varphi(x_{C_1},\dots)\iff\B_\lambda\models
\varphi^\I(C_1,\dots)$ for valid codes, independently of the representatives.

For example, a graph code $f$ on $\delta\times\epsilon$, embedded in the square
of width $\theta=\delta+\epsilon+1$, uses
\[
 F(i,j)\iff i<\delta\land j<\epsilon\land\Bit(f,p_\theta(i,j)).
\]
It is a total function exactly when
\[
 \forall i<\delta\ \exists j<\epsilon\,
 \bigl(F(i,j)\land\forall k<\epsilon\ (F(i,k)\longrightarrow k=j)\bigr).
\]
Injectivity is
$\forall i,i'<\delta\ \forall j<\epsilon\,(F(i,j)\land F(i',j)\to i=i')$,
and surjectivity is $\forall j<\epsilon\,\exists i<\delta\,F(i,j)$.
Occurrences of $f(i)$ in the main text can therefore be eliminated by these
unique-value clauses. Root preservation and edge correspondence add only
finitely many quantifiers. The graph code's birthday is $\Lambda(\theta)$; all
plus positions outside the relevant rectangle can be required absent, which
makes canonical relation coding a definable operation when the intended relation
is specified by a formula and its bit string exists.

The dependency order of the abbreviations is
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}P{0.2\textwidth}P{0.36\textwidth}P{0.34\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Predicate & Earlier definitions needed & Quantification simulated\\
\midrule
$\Ord(a)$ & $\bd(a)=a$ & Embedded ordinals\\
$\Pre(x,y)$ & $<,\bd$ & Literal prefix test\\
$\Bit(s,a)$ & $\Ord,\Pre,<,\bd$ & One sign position\\
$E_C$ & $\Bit,p_\delta$ & Edges of a code\\
$\Code(C)$ & shape, clean bits, \eqref{hset:eq:extensional}--\eqref{hset:eq:rooted} & Pointed membership graphs; all subsets of one domain\\
$\sim$, $\in_\I$ & $\Code$, $E$, coded maps & Interpreted equality and membership\\
$\OC(a)$, $\Vis$ & $\Ord$, $\Bit$, $p_\theta$, $\sim$ & Literal ordinal as a set code\\
$\Coll$ & $\Code$, $\in_\I$, $\Vis$ & Collection of literal ordinals\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The table follows source 02's ledger, with this report's names. A tuple variable
is eliminated by replacing it with ordinary variables; ``exactly one'' has its
usual two-quantifier expansion; variables are renamed before substitution to
prevent capture. The specification is an ordinary finite formula, albeit a long
one.

\subsection{Every potentially large construction has a stated bound}

\begin{longtable}{@{}P{0.35\textwidth}P{0.56\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Object & Bound used in the proof\\
\midrule\endhead
Subsets tested in well-foundedness & Individual sign strings of birthday $\delta$; no collection of that whole fiber is required.\\[0.4em]
A relation on a code domain $\delta$ & Sign birthday $\Lambda(\delta)=\delta^2+\delta<\lambda$.\\[0.4em]
An isomorphism or member embedding & Square width $\delta+\epsilon+1<\lambda$, with the corresponding $\Lambda$ bound.\\[0.4em]
Collapse value of a rooted code & Transitive closure has exactly $\card\delta$ members.\\[0.4em]
An arithmetic computation & At most $\mu$ word vertices or table cells, hereditary size $\mu$, where $\mu$ bounds the inputs and is infinite.\\[0.4em]
Rank labels in that computation & Below $\mu^+$, hence each has cardinality at most $\mu$.\\[0.4em]
Replacement image after eternity & One bound $\max(\aleph_0,\card\delta,\card\varrho)<\kappa$, not an arbitrary singular-cardinal union.\\[0.4em]
A packed birthday-$\alpha$ fiber & Exists exactly if $2^{\card\alpha}<\kappa(\lambda)$.\\[0.4em]
The old-power-set parameter & Hereditary size $(2^{\card\alpha})^M$ for infinite $\alpha$.\\[0.4em]
Actual power set of a set of hereditary size $\mu$ & At most $2^\mu$; this need not be below $\kappa$, and no earlier row needs it to be.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\section{An essential negative regression}
\label{hset:app:regression}

In the membership definition, edge preservation alone is insufficient.
Take the code for $x=\varnothing$ and the three-node chain coding
$y=\{\{\varnothing\}\}$. Map the sole node of the first code to the middle
node of the chain, which codes $\{\varnothing\}$. The image root is a child
of the target root, and the one-node induced subgraph has no edges, just
like the source. But $\varnothing\notin\{\{\varnothing\}\}$.
The predecessor-full clause \eqref{hset:eq:member-full} rejects this false
witness, because the middle node has a predecessor omitted from the image.
The finite suites of sources 06, 07 and 09 confirm this counterexample.

\section{A small concrete membership code}
\label{hset:app:example}

Let $x=\{\varnothing,\{\varnothing\}\}$, the von Neumann ordinal $2$ (source 02's
example). Its transitive closure with the root included has the three nodes
$0\leftrightarrow\varnothing$, $1\leftrightarrow\{\varnothing\}$,
$2\leftrightarrow x$. Take $\delta=3$, root $t=2$, and a relation word of length
$\Lambda(3)=12$. The edges are $(0,1)$, $(0,2)$ and $(1,2)$; under
$p_3(u,v)=3u+v$ the plus positions are $1,2,5$, and all other positions of the
length-12 word are minus. Its collapse is the identity on the ordinal $3$, with
root value $2$.

A different labeling can put $x$ at label $0$, the empty set at label $2$, and
$\{\varnothing\}$ at label $1$. The relation word then changes, and the root is no
longer the largest label. Nevertheless the isomorphism code identifies the two
roots, so interpreted equality cannot be literal equality of the triples. The
example also shows why label order must not be confused with membership order in
the countable-ordinal overflow of \cref{hset:cor:countable}.
